戰爭紅利及轉投資挹注，2Q26 稅後 EPS 上修 445%至 0.99 元 
戰爭紅利推升報價及低價庫存效應，預估 2Q26營收上修 1.0%至 461.84億
元，季增 10%，年增 1.8%，營業利益上修至 5.89億元，虧轉盈，業外轉投
資台塑化獲利貢獻上修至 46.18億元，台塑美國獲利貢獻提升至 30億元，
稅後 EPS上修 445%至 0.99元，季增 93.3%，遠優於 2Q25之-1.04元。台
塑美國具美國乙烷供應穩定且成本低廉優勢，搶占市場，帶動獲利改善。 
預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 上修 117.3%至 0.75 元 
展望 3Q26 荷姆茲海峽解封，原料供應恢復，開工率提升，產量增加，另油
價跌至起漲點，回補庫存買盤支撐，預期油價及塑化產品報價止跌回穩，搭
配傳統旺季，需求增溫，預估 3Q26 營收上修 3.0%至 464.15 億元，季增
0.5%，年增 11.3%，營業利益 4.61億元，季減 21.7%，遠優於 3Q25虧損
26.85 億元，業外轉投資台塑化獲利貢獻上修至 31.03 億元，台塑美國獲利
貢獻推估約 20億元，稅後 EPS上修 117.3%至 0.75元，季減 24.7%，遠優
於 3Q25之-0.09元。3Q26獲利季減反映戰爭紅利消失，利差收斂，惟中東
石化產能受損(約占全球產能比重 15%)，維繫台塑本業及台塑美國仍可獲利。 
戰爭紅利提振獲利，培育電子化學事業小金雞，展望正向 
受惠戰爭紅利推升報價及低價庫存貢獻，提振營運，預估 2026 年營收上修
3.0%至 1821.58 億元，年增 3.8%，營業利益上修至 3.89 億元，業外轉投
資台塑化獲利貢獻上修至 160.89億元，台塑美國獲利貢獻上修至 58.5億元，
稅後 EPS上修 280.5%至 2.74元(原估 0.72元)，遠優於 2025年每股虧損
1.58元。而台塑擴大投資電子化學品，培育小金雞，受惠供應鏈在地化政策，
成長明確，另受惠轉投資南亞科股價大漲，推估台塑 2026 年底淨值增至
63.97 元，目前本淨比約 0.82 倍，評價偏低，維持買進評等，目標價由 65
元上調至 70元(以 1.1 PBR X 2026年底淨值 63.97元評價)。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
戰爭紅利及低價庫存效益，與轉投資獲利挹注，預估 2Q26 稅後 EPS
上修 445%至 0.99 元，季增 93.3%，獲利大幅改善。 
⧫  
中東產能受損，維繫利差水準，預估 3Q26/2026 稅後 EPS 各上修
117.3%/280.5%至 0.75 元/2.74 元，擺脫 2025 年虧損狀態。 
⧫  
戰爭紅利提振獲利，培育電子化學事業小金雞， 維持買進， 目標價由
65 元上調至 70 元(以 1.1 PBR X 2026 年底淨值 63.97 元評價)。

營運分析  
轉投資台塑化獲利挹注，台塑 1Q26 稅後 EPS 增至 0.51 元，由虧轉盈 
台塑 1Q26營收 419.85億元(原估 392.54億元)，季增 2.1%，年減 11.1%，優於預期 7.0%，乃因
農曆年假工作天數較少，且亦有 15 座工廠進行歲修，1 月及 2 月產銷量滑落，不過美伊戰爭催動
油價上漲成本推升，加以斷料風險提高，客戶積極回補庫存，3月出貨量增溫，石化產品 3 月均價
也較 2 月上漲 9 至 24%不等，帶動整季均價上漲 7%至 14%，維繫營收表現。營益率則季增
2.7ppt至-3.1%(原估-4.1%)，營業虧損 13.02億元(原估虧損 16.15億元)，雖連 7季虧損，但虧損
見到收斂。業外轉投資台塑化貢獻大增至 56.9億元，且台塑美國虧損收斂至 1.53億元，提振稅後
EPS至 0.51元(原估-0.08元)，由虧轉盈，也優於 1Q25之 0.01元。台塑本業仍處虧損，乃因 1月
2月市況欠佳，加以歲修干擾拖累，不過 3月產品報價上揚，提振獲利表現，整季虧損逐步收斂。

戰爭紅利及低價庫存效益，以及轉投資獲利挹注，預估 2Q26 稅後 EPS 上修
445%至 0.99 元，季增 93.3%，獲利大幅改善 
進入 2Q26，受惠美伊戰爭紅利，台塑旗下產品報價於 4月大幅上漲，抵銷缺料干擾，提振 4月營
收 183.26億元，月增 5.6%，年增 13.3%，儘管 5月產品報價變化不大，惟缺料影響浮現，出貨量
減少，拖累 5月營收 149.51億元，月減 18.4%，年增 0.7%。進入 6月，荷姆茲海峽封鎖造成缺料
干擾消退，乙烯及丙烯等原料供應比預期穩健，產量回穩，惟油價下跌拖累產品報價弱勢，預期 6
月營收將延續月減。整體而言，戰爭紅利推升產品報價，預估 2Q26營收上修 1.0%至 461.84億元
(原估 457.31億元)，季增 10%，年增 1.8%，營益率上修 1.2ppt至 1.3%(原估 0.1%)，季增 4.4ppt，
年增 4.7ppt，營業利益上修至 5.89億元(原估 0.34億元)，單季虧轉盈，也優於 2Q25之虧損 15.63
億元，業外轉投資台塑化獲利貢獻上修至 46.18 億元(原估 13.62 億元)，而台塑美國則順利由虧轉
盈，預估獲利貢獻提升至 30億元，稅後 EPS上修 445%至 0.99元(原估 0.18元)，季增 93.3%，
遠優於 2Q25之-1.04元，獲利大幅改善。2Q26營益率季增，係因戰爭紅利，加上低價原料庫存效
應，利差於 4月及 5月維持高檔，獲利增溫，而業外轉投資方面，台塑化煉油獲利貢獻穩健，至於
台塑美國由虧轉盈，係荷姆茲海峽封鎖，亞洲國家缺料減產，塑化產品報價高檔，而美國乙烷供應
穩定，且成本低廉，利差擴張，並伺機擴大出貨量及搶占市場，帶動獲利大幅改善。

中東產能受損，維繫產品利差，預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 上修 117.3%至 0.75 元 
展望 3Q26，美伊和談進展，荷姆茲海峽可望逐步解封，預期原料供應逐漸恢復，台塑開工率可望
由 2Q26約 60-65%提升至 80%，產量增加，另一方面近期油價大幅下跌造成市場觀望，塑化產品
報價同樣呈現弱勢修正，由於油價已經回落至美伊戰前的起漲點附近，回補庫存買盤可望為油價形
成支撐，預期油價將呈現整理格局，有望維繫塑化產品報價止跌回穩，搭配 3Q26 為傳統旺季，需
求增溫，提振出貨量，預估 3Q26營收上修 3.0%至 464.15億元(原估 450.45億元)，季增 0.5%，
年增 11.3%，營益率下修 0.2ppt 至 1.0%(原估 1.2%)，季減 0.3ppt，年增 7.4ppt，營業利益下修
14.3%至 4.61億元(原估 5.38億元)，季減 21.7%，遠優於 3Q25虧損 26.85億元，業外轉投資方
面，推估台塑化獲利貢獻上修至 31.03億元(原估 15.12億元)，以及台塑美國獲利貢獻推估約 20億
元(原估虧損 3億元)，稅後 EPS上修 117.3%至 0.75元(原估 0.34元)，季減 24.7%，遠優於 3Q25
之-0.09元。3Q26營益率季減，台塑美國獲利貢獻減少，乃因戰爭紅利消失，利差收斂，惟中東石
化產能受損(約占全球產能比重 15%)，台塑本業仍可維持獲利狀態，台塑美國亦可貢獻穩健獲利。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 6月 29日 Formosa Plastics 第4 頁，共 17頁  
中東產能受損維繫台塑本業產品利差，加上轉投資台塑化及台塑美國獲利提升之挹
注，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 上修 280.5%至 2.74 元，由虧轉盈 
展望 2026 年，美伊戰爭一度引爆中東政局動盪，不過美伊和談進展，荷姆茲海峽解封，原油供應
逐漸恢復，油價回落，成本減輕，而中東石化產能受損，有助於改善全球供需，預期塑化產品利差
水準仍可高於 2月美伊戰前的低迷水準，有利 2H26維持獲利狀態，另烏俄停戰走向和談仍是大勢
所趨，俄羅斯無須再以折扣價格銷給中國煉化廠商，中國煉化廠商成本較低的優勢將流失，加上中
國政府反內捲政策推進，淘汰老舊落後產能，整頓石化產業秩序，中國價格競爭壓力減輕，亦可期
待烏克蘭重建，提振 PVC 需求，有利台塑本業獲利改善，加上轉投資台塑化及台塑美國獲利提升，
預估 2026年營收上修 3.0%至 1821.58億元(原估 1,768.79億元)，年增 3.8%，營益率上修 0.4ppt
至 0.2%(原估-0.2%)，營業利益上修至 3.89 億元(原估虧損 3.65 億元)，業外轉投資台塑化獲利上
修至 160.89億元(原估 50.84億元)之貢獻，台塑美國獲利貢獻上修至 58.5億元(原估虧損 12.5億
元)，稅後 EPS上修 280.5%至 2.74元(原估 0.72元)，遠優於 2025年每股虧損 1.58元，年底淨值
63.97元(原估 55.24元)。

南亞科(2408)股價波動劇烈，先保守以 1Q26 底收盤價作為評價基礎，推估台塑
2026 年底淨值 63.97 元 
台塑藉由”透過其他綜合損益按公允價值衡量之金融資產 -非流動”會計科目持有南亞科(2408)股權約
10.81%，每季底依據南亞科股價變動造成的持股市值變化將反映在台塑之股權權益及淨值。台塑
分別於 2025/10/28、11/11及 12/31公告相繼已處分 2.5萬、1.8萬及約 1.9萬張南亞科持股，平
均處分價格各為 98.81元、145.23元及 169.47元，台塑持有南亞科股權降至 8.81%。簡單試算，
若南亞科股價每變動 10元，將造成台塑淨值變動約 0.43元。 
若以南亞科最新股價(2026/6/26)449元計算，對比 2026/3/31收盤價 198.5元，台塑持股 8.81%的
評價利益將較 1Q26 底再增加約 683.47 億元，可貢獻台塑淨值增加約 10.74 元。而台塑於
2026/3/6公告將再處分南亞科股權 2%，約 6.2萬張，處分期間至 2026/12/31，目前已處分約 7千
張。若完全處分完畢，台塑持有南亞科股權將降至 6.81%。由於南亞科股價波動較為劇烈，南亞科
處分狀況也尚未底定，因此先以持股 8.81%以及保守以 2026/3/31收盤價作為評價基礎，推估台塑
2026年底淨值為 63.97元。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 6月 29日 Formosa Plastics 第5 頁，共 17頁
台塑擴大投資電子化學品，培育小金雞，受惠供應鏈在地化政策，成長趨勢明確
為了降低中國石化廠商擴充產能的競爭壓力，台塑近幾年開始進行轉型投資計畫，擴大投資電子化
學品研發與生產，培育小金雞，擴建計畫較積極的主要是兩家子公司台塑德山及台塑大金。台塑持
有台塑德山 50%股權，主要產品為電子級異丙醇(IPA)，年產能 3 萬噸，也配合客戶要求進行廢液
回收業務，預計 3Q26機械設備完工後，將開始向客戶回收廢液，進行加工處理後，再將產品回銷
給客戶。台塑德山配合客戶供應鏈在地化政策，將再擴充二期廠房，年產能規劃 3 萬噸，預計
2027 年底完工，2028 年中完成認證後即可開始出貨。台塑德山對台塑的獲利貢獻 2025 年提升至
1.2 億元，優於 2024 年虧損 2000 萬元，乃受惠客戶先進製程產能提升擴大對異丙醇需求，1Q26
獲利貢獻降至 1368 萬元，較 4Q25 獲利 3945 萬元，大幅萎縮，乃異丙醇原料丙烯價格大漲，成

本墊高，台塑德山尚且無法完全轉嫁，導致獲利萎縮，預估 2Q26獲利仍顯疲軟，而 3Q26開始可
以轉嫁成本，預計獲利將可改善。而 2028年二期廠新產能開出，可持續帶動獲利增長。
另外台塑也持有台塑大金 50%股權，主要產品為氫氟酸，年產能 4.5萬噸，亦有氟化銨 7800噸以
及緩衝級氫氟酸 1800噸，目前仍有產能擴充規劃，包括氫氟酸 6500噸以及氟化銨 2800噸，預計
2027 年陸續完成。過往營運模式為將氫氟酸銷售給日本客戶，經過加工處理再銷售給主要客戶，
目前台塑大金已可將部分產品直接銷售給主要客戶，可提升獲利率。 2025 年台塑大金獲利貢獻
9800萬元，年增 82.5%，1Q26獲利為 2478萬元，年增 56.2%，表現穩健。對比台塑塑化本業營
收規模，台塑旗下兩家電子化學品子公司整體營收貢獻不顯著，但獲利已逐年改善，隨著主要客戶
的供應鏈在地化政策推進，台塑兩家子公司持續擴產，營收規模及獲利亦將逐步擴大，成長趨勢明
確。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 6月 29日 Formosa Plastics 第6 頁，共 17頁  
產業概況  
美伊啟動和談，拖累油價下挫，波段跌幅超過 30% 
美國與伊朗於 2026年 4月初開始啟動停火協議談判，經歷過 2個多月斡旋及拉鋸，於 2026/6/12
雙方針對停火條件達成共識，並於 6/17正式簽署包含 14個要點諒解備忘錄 MOU，承諾在 60天內
達成最終全面協議。在美伊進行停火談判期間，荷姆茲海峽仍處於封鎖狀態，全球原油供應仍有短
缺，不過 OECD 主要國家以原油庫存支應需求，加上美伊和談的預期心理，油價即面臨震盪拉回
的修正壓力，期間美伊談判反覆幾度再推動油價反彈，但都未能突破 3月高點，呈現高檔整理格局，
隨著美伊雙方談判進展順利，局勢趨於明朗，油價則於 5月初高點 100美元以上，一路修正至近期
波段低點，西德州原油跌破 70 美元，布蘭特原油跌破 75 美元，波段跌幅均超過 30%，走勢疲弱，
已經逼近 2026/2/28美伊戰爭爆發前的油價區間 65-70美元。 
圖 4：西德州與布蘭特原油價格走勢圖(美元/桶)

資料來源：Platts、元大投顧整理

OECD 可望回補庫存，加上 3Q26 旺季需求支撐，油價可望築底整理，伺機反彈 
另一方面，觀察 OECD 國家原油庫存趨勢，美伊戰爭開打後，各國政府為因應荷姆茲海峽封鎖造
成的原油供應短缺，先提供原油庫存滿足原油需求，造成原油庫存於近 4 個月庫存大減 2.49 億桶
至 25.59億桶，為 2024年 2月以來新低，也低於過去 4年平均的 27.7億桶。由於荷姆茲海峽解封，
中東產油國原油運輸恢復，供應趨於穩定，各國政府有動機於油價相對低檔之際，進行原油庫存回
補，可為油價形成支撐，展望 3Q26 為汽油消費的傳統旺季，且 2026 年聖嬰效應顯著，天氣炎熱，
柴油發電需求有機會隨之增加，提振整體原油需求及油價表現，因此預期油價有機會在 70-75美元
之間築底整理，伺機反彈。

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 6月 29日 Formosa Plastics 第7 頁，共 17頁  
圖 5：西德州與布蘭特原油價格走勢圖(美元/桶)

資料來源：Platts、元大投顧整理

亞洲廠商缺料減產的乙烯產能損失缺口約 16-17%，供需過剩格局趨於改善 
荷姆茲海峽封鎖期間，原油運輸中斷，高度依賴中東原料供應的亞洲煉油及石化廠商面臨缺料狀況，
包括日本、韓國、台灣、中國及東南亞等均有石化廠商相繼宣布不可抗力因素而減產，調降開工率。
以乙烯產能為例，亞洲乙烯產能佔全球比重約 45%，若因缺料而將開工率由原本正常的 75-80%水
準調降至 50%左右，推估一個月乙烯產能損失造成的乙烯供需缺口約 12.5%，加計中東約 15%乙
烯產能受損而運作不順(假設開工率僅 20%)，就算美洲(包括美國及南美)的石化廠商會伺機拉高開
工率填補，推估全球乙烯一個月實際的缺口約 16-17%。至於其他基礎石化原料因缺料及運作不順
的產能損失大概落在 14-17%不等。整體而言，缺料造成減產的供給限縮，有助於加速庫存去化，
塑化產品供需過剩格局趨於改善。

### Page 8

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 6月 29日 Formosa Plastics 第8 頁，共 17頁  
圖 6：全球石化基礎原料因缺料減產的產能損失推估比率彙整表

資料來源：元大投顧整理

中東受損的石化產能需要時間修復，而 3Q26 傳統旺季，有利報價反彈回穩 
美伊停火談判導致近一個多月油價回落重挫，而下游產品買氣縮手，市場陷入觀望，拖累塑化產品
報價同步修正。隨著荷姆茲海峽解封，原油供應可望逐漸恢復，亞洲石化廠商缺料緩解，開工率可
望逐步提升，產出增加，而佔全球石化產能比重約 15%的中東地區，因戰爭受損運作停擺之產能
需要較長時間的修復，預期供給端擴張幅度可控，而 3Q26 則是傳統塑化需求旺季，預期銷售規模
回升，有利塑化產品報價及利差反彈回穩。

中國反內捲及烏俄停戰等利多，預期 2026 年塑化景氣轉折向上 
隨著美伊和談進展，戰爭衝擊逐漸淡化，而多數塑化產品報價一度大漲，利差好轉，提振塑化廠商
獲利表現。近期戰爭紅利消退，報價回落，利差萎縮，不過多數塑化產品可擺脫 2025年多處於虧
損或損平邊緣的狀態。我們認為目前處於塑化產業景氣轉折的關鍵時刻，有幾個可能為未來帶來
「曙光」的發展契機。觀察面向包括以下幾點，包括 1) 中國乙烷裂解乙烯新產能投資計畫恐生變
數；2)中國淘汰老舊產能，改善供需失衡格局；3) 烏俄戰爭若能停戰，可削減中國低價原油成本優
勢，減緩市場競爭壓力。預期 2026年塑化產業景氣轉折向上。

### Page 9

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 6月 29日 Formosa Plastics 第9 頁，共 17頁  
獲利調整與股票評價  
戰爭紅利提振本業獲利加上轉投資台塑化及台塑美國獲利貢獻，2026 年重啟成長 
展望 2026 年，美伊戰爭一度引爆中東政局動盪，不過美伊和談進展，荷姆茲海峽解封，原油供應
逐漸恢復，油價回落，成本減輕，而中東石化產能受損，有助於改善全球供需，預期塑化產品利差
水準仍可高於 2月美伊戰前的低迷水準，有利 2H26維持獲利狀態，另烏俄停戰走向和談仍是大勢
所趨，俄羅斯無須再以折扣價格銷給中國煉化廠商，中國煉化廠商成本較低的優勢將流失，加上中
國政府反內捲政策推進，淘汰老舊落後產能，整頓石化產業秩序，中國價格競爭壓力減輕，亦可期
待烏克蘭重建，提振 PVC 需求，有利台塑本業獲利改善，加上轉投資台塑化及台塑美國獲利提升，
預估 2026年營收上修 3.0%至 1821.58億元(原估 1,768.79億元)，年增 3.9%，營益率上修 0.4ppt
至 0.2%(原估-0.2%)，營業利益上修至 3.89 億元(原估虧損 3.65 億元)，業外轉投資台塑化獲利上
修至 160.89億元(原估 50.84億元)之貢獻，台塑美國獲利貢獻上修至 58.5億元(原估虧損 12.5億
元)，稅後 EPS上修 280.5%至 2.74元(原估 0.72元)，遠優於 2025年每股虧損 1.58元，年底淨值
63.97元(原估 55.24元)。 
考量: 1) 受惠戰爭紅利及低價庫存貢獻，預估台塑 2Q26營業利益 5.89億元，單季虧轉盈，本業營
運好轉，加計轉投資台塑化及台塑美國獲利貢獻，稅後 EPS上修 444.9%至 0.99元，季增 93.3%，
遠優於 2Q25 之-1.04 元，獲利大幅改善。2)中東產能受損，預估台塑旗下產品利差可優於美伊戰
前的低迷水準，預估 3Q26 營業利益 4.61 億元，遠優於 3Q25虧損 26.85 億元，稅後 EPS 上修
117.3%至 0.75元，遠優於 3Q25之-0.09元。3)烏俄和談仍是大勢所趨，中國煉化廠商成本較低的
優勢將流失，價格競爭壓力減輕，且可期待烏克蘭重建提振 PVC需求，預估 2026年稅後 EPS上
修 280.5%至 2.74元，重啟成長軌道。4) 台塑擴大投資電子化學品，培育小金雞，受惠供應鏈在地
化政策，成長趨勢明確。5)受惠轉投資南亞科股價大漲，推估台塑 2026 年底淨值增至 63.97 元，
目前本淨比約 0.82 倍，評價偏低，維持買進評等，目標價由 65 元上調至 70 元(以 1.1 PBR X 
2026年底淨值 63.97元評價)。

公司簡介  
台塑(1301)成立於 1954年，一開始設立 PVC廠，並往上游原料電石、VCM、液鹼、聚丙烯腈纖維、聚乙烯及其他石化原
料等產品線發展，1980 年成立台塑美國公司(FPC-USA)，1992 年轉投資成立台塑石化公司，1993 年興建第六輕油裂解廠，
配合集團石化原料上下一貫生產的垂直整合策略，於麥寮廠設立 PVC、AE，LLDPE、AN等工廠。2002年成立台塑工業
(寧波)有限公司，於寧波廠設立之 PVC、AE、高吸水性樹脂及 PP產線，完成台塑在兩岸的生產佈局。主要營業項目為塑
膠及纖維原料產銷，其中 VCM及 PVC兩項產品年產能為全球第三大廠，至於其他產品線液鹼、丙烯酸、正丁醇、高吸水
性樹脂、丙烯腈及環氧氯丙烷產能也是名列世界前茅。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 台塑整體的 ESG風險評級屬於中等風險，於 Sustainalytics資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於略微落後
的位置，亦於大宗化學品行業的公司中排名落後同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 台塑的整體曝險屬於中等水準，且略落後於大宗化學品行業平均水準。該公司整體風險曝險為
中等，與行業平均相近。產品和服務的環境和社會影響、自身運作的碳排放、廢水和廢棄物處理是值得關注的重大 ESG
議題。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：台塑在針對重大 ESG議題的管理水準和執行力為平均水準。該公司在環境管理方面
表現穩健。然而，其高階主管薪酬並未與 ESG績效目標明確掛鉤。台塑致力於減少溫室氣體排放，目標是到 2030年在
2020年的基礎上減排 40%，並在 2050年實現氣候中和。為實現這一目標，台塑已升級設備並擴大了再生能源的使用。
同時，台塑也意識到塑膠帶來的風險，並擴大了永續材料的應用以應對這一問題。然而，該公司 2024財年來自永續發
展相關產品的收入不足 5%，顯示對永續產品的關注有限。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

- 1 - 
個股報告 
台塑(1301) 
本業轉虧為盈，業外轉投資助益 
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 $62.5 
前次投資建議 2025.12.29： 
「逢低買進」 ，目標價: $50

報告日期：2026.6.29 研 究 員：陳泂暉

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ PVC受高庫存與和談觀望影響，短期買盤保守、美廠歲修；未來仍持續受到中國新產能開出
壓抑。乙烯供應回升帶動報價大跌，利於EVA成本改善；惟受美伊和談以及下游產品高價影
響，短期需求偏弱。 
➢ 考量本業轉虧為盈，業外轉投資助益，推升評價回升，投資評等維持「逢低買進」，6 個月
目標價 62.5(1X26(F)PBR)，潛在上漲空間 19.7%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/6/26 收盤價： 52.20 ➢ 公司 26Q1 營 收 為419.85 億 元 ，QoQ+2.1% 、
YoY-11.1%，營收較去年同期衰退，主因受2 至 3月麥寮
廠區安排停車歲修，疊加農曆過年使得交易天數較少影
響；毛利率為 3.7%，QoQ+1.3ppts、YoY-1.5ppts，因部
分主力產線歲修使得產能稼動率下滑，且3月起原料成本
高漲壓縮了 EVA 與特化等產品之毛利表現；稅後淨利
32.69 億元，較上季虧轉盈、YoY+6,379.2%；稅後 EPS 
0.51 元。 
➢ PVC 受高庫存與和談觀望影響，短期買盤保守、美廠歲
修；未來仍持續受到中國新產能開出壓抑。 
➢ 乙烯供應回升帶動報價大跌，利於 EVA 成本改善；惟受
美伊和談以及下游產品高價影響，短期需求偏弱。 
➢ 預估 2026 年營收 1,706.79 億元，YoY-2.7%；毛利率
6.6%，YoY+3.4ppts；稅後淨利 110.6億元，較去年虧轉
盈；稅後 EPS 1.74元。

台塑目前股價位於 0.8X26(F)PBR，位於公司過往0.7-1.7X
區間下緣位置，考量本業轉虧為盈，業外轉投資助益 ，推
升評價回升，所以取中下緣 1X，給予「逢低買進」投資評
等，6 個月目標價 62.5(1X2026(F)PBR)，潛在上漲空間
19.7%。

營 運 分 析

台塑為亞洲最大 PVC生產商之一 
台灣塑膠工業股份有限公司成立於 1954 年，主要經營業務為塑膠原料、塑
膠加工、精密化學及纖維製品等，其中 PVC以及 VCM年產能為全球第三大，
其他塑化產品產能也位居前列。除了原有塑化業務外，集團內也有進行轉投
資，集團旗下事業包括塑膠、紡織、石化、電子、能源、運輸、工務、教育、
生物科技、醫療等領域。26Q1產品營收比重為: PVC 20%、PP 15%、Acrylic 
Esters 8%、液鹼 8%、LLDPE 8%、EVA 8%、HDPE 7%、AN 5%、SAP 4%、
MMA 2%、NBA 2%、MTBE 2%、其他 11%。 
         圖一、台塑產品供應鏈(藍色主要為自用、紅色為外賣)

資料來源：台塑、兆豐國際整理

- 3 - 
個股報告 
戰爭推升成本擠壓毛利，核心產品呈現價量分化 
公司 26Q1營收為 419.85 億元，QoQ+2.1%、YoY-11.1%，營收較去年同期
衰退，主因受 2至 3月麥寮廠區安排停車歲修，疊加農曆過年使得交易天數
較少影響，導致部分主力產品出貨量明顯萎縮；價格方面，受 2 月底戰爭爆
發引發原物料成本推升影響，多數主力產品（如 HDPE、LDPE、AN、正丁
醇、碳纖維）報價呈現調漲或持平。此外，3 月份起原油及乙、丙烯等原料
價格因戰爭關係突發高漲，疊加關稅問題衝擊液鹼產品需求，惟下游客戶因
擔憂短缺而積極進場採購 PE 產品，進而造成部分核心產品呈現價量齊揚；
毛利率為 3.7%，QoQ+1.3ppts、YoY-1.5ppts，因部分主力產線歲修使得產
能稼動率下滑，且 3 月起原料成本高漲壓縮了 EVA 與特化等產品之毛利表
現；稅後淨利 32.69 億元，較上季虧轉盈、YoY+6,379.2%；稅後 EPS 0.51
元。

高庫存以及高價壓制 PVC的 Q3買盤，陸廠中國新產能開出壓抑市況表現 
PVC產業面臨買盤保守與高庫存去化的短期挑戰。在市場需求端，由於印度
客戶先前自中國大陸大量採購低價 PVC，導致目前的社會庫存推升至 60 萬
噸左右，顯著高於 40至 50萬噸的常態水位；同時，隨著美伊和談推進引發
原料與產品跌價預期，下游客戶在短期內將維持保守觀望，壓制短期市場需
求。在供給與營運端，台塑美國的 PVC 部分廠區與產線安排在 7 月至 8 月
的夏季進行例行性停車歲修，加上產品價格隨亞洲市況修正，其第三季的開
工率與獲利將較第二季高峰下滑，但持續維持在獲利狀態。

展望長線全球與中國大陸的 PVC產業將進入產能調節與供需重組階段 。中國
大陸市場由於先前 PVC產能過剩嚴重，下半年的新產能投放量相對較少，新
增供給壓力主要集中於其他烯烴系列產品。另外，過去 1至 2年大量已完工
的產線因市況不佳或原料短缺而主動延後投產，因此實質新增產能對市場造
成的實際衝擊將小於明面上的帳面數字。

圖二、聚氯乙烯 PVC利差(乙烯法)

資料來源：Platts、兆豐國際整理、單位:美元/噸

- 4 - 
個股報告 
圖三、全球 PVC供需以及預估

資料來源：IHS、兆豐國際整理、單位:萬噸

荷姆茲海峽重啟帶動成本紅利，下游客戶跌價預期制約短期補庫意願 
EVA產業在成本端，隨著中東荷姆茲海峽重啟開放，主要原料乙烯全球供應
量回升，且台灣中油的原料供應量在經歷五月故障後已恢復至 100%滿載，
帶動乙烯市場報價大幅下跌；這對於第一季因戰爭引發乙烯成本驟增而導致
毛利壓縮的 EVA產品而言，具有明確的成本控管與效益回升助益。然而在需
求端，受美伊和談推進所引發的跌價預期心理影響，包括EVA在內的石化產
品下游客戶買盤，普遍維持保守觀望心態，導致短期內的整體市場需求動能
偏弱。

圖四、乙烯醋酸乙烯脂共聚物 EVA利差

資料來源：Platts、兆豐國際整理、單位:美元/噸

台塑規劃處分 6萬多張南亞科，目前已執行約一成 
公司董事會先前已通過將再出售 2%的南亞科持股，持股比例預計自 8.81%
降至 6.81%。本次預計處分總量約 6萬多張，執行期間自 3月 6日董事會通
過起至今年年底。目前僅執行約一成出頭（處分約 7,000 多張） 。鑑於南亞科
上半年營運表現優異、超乎市場預期，公司將採取配合市價「慢慢賣」的策
略，此項資產活化將對公司的現金流與資金調度帶來顯著助益。

- 5 - 
個股報告 
預估台塑碳費一年支出約 1.59億元，影響低於原先預期 
由於台塑屬於高碳洩漏產業，需在 2025 年 6 月底前向環境部提交自主減量
計畫。若該計畫順利通過審查，預計碳費將從原先的 300 元/噸降至 20 元/
噸。根據台塑先前披露的數據，2022 年其碳排放量為 764.3 萬噸，因此估算
碳費支出將從每年 23.8 億元大幅下降至 1.59 億元，對應影響稅前EPS由原
先約 0.4元減至 0.02 元。

本業轉虧為盈，業外轉投資助益，推升評價回升 
展望 26Q2 營收預估為 467.34 億元，QoQ+11.3%、YoY+3%，季增因受戰
爭影響，產品報價上升；毛利率 7.9%，QoQ+4.2ppts、YoY+4.4ppts，因產
能利用率回升；稅後淨損 43.82 億元，QoQ+34.1%、較去年同期虧轉盈；稅
後 EPS 0.69 元。

預估 2026年營收 1,706.79 億元，YoY-2.7%；毛利率 6.6%，YoY+3.4ppts；
稅後淨利 110.6億元，較去年虧轉盈；稅後 EPS 1.74 元。

台塑目前股價位於 0.8X26(F)PBR，位於公司過往 0.7-1.7X 區間下緣位置，
考量本業轉虧為盈，業外轉投資助益，推升評價回升，所以取中下緣 1X，給
予「逢低買進」投資評等，6個月目標價 62.5(1X2026(F)PBR)，潛在上漲空
間 19.7%。 
本業轉虧為盈，業外
轉投資助益，推升 評
價回升

公司轉型成果逐步開始顯現 
 維持買進， 目標價75 元：投顧認為隨今年 NB 整體出貨量大幅下滑，
預期 2026 年 NB 相關營收將下滑 30~40%。然濱川泰國廠已在今年
開始生產水冷零件，公司將把重心轉移至擴展高毛利的水冷散熱產
品，預期將會在 3Q26 開始量產，投顧預期濱川 2026 年營收將成長
20%， 毛利率提升至19%下 ， 估計2026 年 EPS 1.4 元，2027 年 EPS 
5.1 元，維持買進建議，目標價 75 元。 
 1Q26 毛利率回升， EPS-0.73 元：濱川 1Q26 營收 8.06 億元，
+6%QOQ，-1.5%YOY。獲利方面，毛利率升至 7.1%，較去年同期
上升 5.9%，主要是因產品組合優化。稅後淨損 1.02 億元，1Q26 稅
後 EPS-0.73 元。 
 積極切入 AI 伺服器水冷散熱領域，預期3Q26 營收回溫：展望 2026
年，投顧預估 2Q26 營收 8.8 億元，+9.2%QOQ，+1%YOY。隨今
年 NB 整體出貨量大幅下滑， 預期 2026 年 NB 相關營收將下滑
30~40%。醫療相關業務以外科醫療器材、手術器械、手術盒等產品
為主 ， 預期醫療業務今明年營收將與去年持平於2 億元左右 。 然濱川
泰國廠已在今年開始生產水冷零件 ， 公司將把重心轉移至擴展高毛利
的 AI 伺服器水冷散熱產品，涵蓋分岐管及水冷快接頭，預期將會在
3Q26 開始量產，水冷業務貢獻將逐步提升，帶動整體營運改善，  
2026 年營收占比可望達 30%以上 ， 投顧預期濱川2026 年營收 39.13
億元，年增 20%。

本刊載之報告為元富投顧於特定日期之分析，已力求陳述內容之可靠性，純屬研究性質，僅作參考，使用者應明瞭內容之時效性，審慎考
量投資風險，並就投資結果自行負責。報告著作權屬元富投顧所有，禁止任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。  
元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (1 15)金管投顧新字第 009 號         Page 2 of 5 
個股報告

濱川 為 專 業 金 屬 沖 壓 廠 ， 目
前仍以 NB、平 板 應 用 為 主 ，
積極切入 水冷 應用領域  
 濱川成立於 1983 年 3 月 30 日，2005 年 7 月掛牌上櫃，其為專業金屬沖壓廠，
以金屬沖壓、電腦數值控制  CNC 及塑膠射出成型等方式，生產資訊及消費性
電子等產品之機構及外觀零件 ， 屬自行開發模具並製造產品之公司 ，生產之產品
係以客戶訂單需求為主， 由客戶提供模具(或產品)之圖面 ， 再經由濱川的模具(或
自動化)部門依圖面製模(或加工程式)， 試產合格後再購置金屬原料進行沖壓加工
(或 CNC)製成產品。 
主要產品為金屬及塑膠的外觀件與內部機構件，主要應用於 NB、平板電腦、醫
療手術器材等。濱川最著名的產品為 NB 鍵盤內部支架(剪刀腳)，全球市佔率超
過三成，透過下游鍵盤廠客戶間接出貨到各大 NB 品牌。濱川的既有強項在金屬
沖壓、CNC 與薄型精密結構件，與水冷散熱中的分歧管與快接頭金屬加工需求
有技術銜接性，若搭配其自動化機器人工廠與國際生產基地，長期有機會在  AI 
水冷散熱供應鏈中取得穩定市佔與成長空間。濱川 2025 年營收比重中，金屬機
殼佔約 76%、鍵盤支架 15%、其他 9%。

1Q26 毛利率回升，EPS-0.73 元 
 濱川 1Q26 營收 8.06 億元 ，+6%QOQ，-1.5%YOY。 獲利方面 ，毛利率升至 7.1%，
較去年同期上升 5.9%，主要是因產品組合優化。稅後淨損 1.02 億元，1Q26 稅
後 EPS-0.73 元。

積極切入 AI 伺 服 器 水 冷 散
熱 領域 ，預期 3 Q 2 6營收回
溫  
 展望 2026 年，投顧預估 2Q26 營收 8.8 億元，+9.2%QOQ，+1%YOY。隨今
年 NB 整體出貨量大幅下滑，預期 2026 年 NB 相關營收將下滑 30~40%。醫療
相關業務以外科醫療器材、手術器械、手術盒等產品為主，主要供應 2 家穩定
的客戶，預期醫療業務今明年營收將與去年持平於 2 億元左右。然濱川泰國廠
已在今年開始生產水冷零件， 公司將把重心轉移至擴展高毛利的AI 伺服器水冷
散熱產品，涵蓋分岐管及水冷快接頭，預期將會在 3Q26 開始量產，水冷業務
貢獻將逐步提升，帶動整體營運改善， 2026 年營收占比可望達 30%以上，且
公司生產線逐步採用自動化，將從 6 月開始縮減人力 ， 將可大幅縮減人事成本，
符合公司持續自動化 、 提高生產穩定度的策略目標 。投顧預期濱川 2026 年營收
39.13 億元，年增 20%。

1 
1598 岱宇(DYACO)

報告日期： 2026/06/29 研 究 員：洪  國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、投 資 建 議

一. 預估 2026 年家用市場營收金額 45 億元 ，YoY+3.3% 
家用市場 1Q26 營收金額 12.69 億元， YoY-4.00% 。觀察 2020 年
疫情帶動家用健身器材需求，經歷 2022~2025 年終端庫存己去化
到相對低檔水準，雖然關稅議題恐影響終端銷費力道，但在各通
路商與品牌商小幅回補庫存 ， 預估2025 年家用健身器材將小幅成
長 2~4%。預 估2026 年家用市場營收金額 45.00 億元 ，YoY+3.3%。

二 . 預估 2026 年商用市場營收金額 16.5 億元，
YoY+47.3%  
商用市場 1Q26 營收金額 3.42 億元， YoY+41.0% 。岱宇 2026 年
在商用市場推出重訓產品 ， 己有美國地區型健身房採用 ， 預期2026
年將開始小量出貨。宏遠預估 2026 年商用市場營收金額 16.5 億
元， YoY+47.3% 。

三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.60 元 
岱宇 2026 年家用市場受惠關稅衝擊淡化 ， 通路商訂單逐漸恢復成
長格局，加上商用市場推出重訓新產品，帶動營收恢復成長，另
外大甲廠處份利益 3Q26 認列，有助岱宇 2026 年營運由虧轉盈。
宏遠預估岱宇 2026 年營收金額 70.00 億元， YoY+14.36% ，預估
營業利益 2.34 億元，虧轉盈，預估稅後盈餘 2.76 億元，虧轉盈，
預估稅後 EPS 1.60 元。

四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 1.40 元 
岱宇 2027 年家用市場將持續優化產品結構為主 ， 商用市場在新重
訓產品持續放量成長帶動下，岱宇 2027 年本業獲利將呈現成長，
惟 2026 年業外處分利益墊高基期，預期 2027 年整體獲利將呈現
衰退 。 宏遠預估岱宇2027 年營收金額 77.00 億元 ，YoY+10.00% ，
預估營業利益 2.96 億元 ，YoY+26.53% ， 預估稅後盈餘2.42 億元 ，
YoY-12.23% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.40 元。

五. 投資建議區間操作，區間價 17~22 元 
岱宇歷史 PER 落在 8~20X，PBR 0.7~6X ，2026 年 ROE7%，PER 
13X ，2027 年 ROE 6% ，PER 15X ，評價己達合理水準，故投資
建議區間操作，區間價 17~22 元。 (10.5~14X2026 年稅後 EPS) 
投資評等：區間操作

貳、公 司 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
岱宇國際股份有限公司成立於 1990 年，資本額 17.91 億元，並於
2016 年掛牌上市，岱宇生產基地位於彰化全興工業區、中國東莞
與中國嘉興三處，岱宇為全台灣最大家用健身器材出口廠商，旗
下品牌包括 SOLE 、Spirit 、XTERRA 、FUEL 等，透過生產、通
路與品牌的垂直整合經營策略，深耕全球醫療復健、家用與商用
健身器材市場。岱宇旗下品牌合作銷售通路涵蓋北美、歐洲、中
國等最大健身器材通路，除美國主要合作夥伴旗下銷售據點站穩
七成市占率，岱宇也積極開拓電商平台銷售之佈局。

二. 岱宇產品線與銷售地區  
岱宇 2025 年銷售地區 ， 美洲61~62%、歐 洲21~22%、亞 洲5~6%、
中國 2~3% 、其他地區 9~10% 。岱宇產品線涵蓋高中低階市場，
包括家用市場 SOLE、 大眾市場XTERRA、FUEL、 商用市場SPIRIT
與 UFC 等。

圖三、岱宇產品佈局

三. 2026 年營運概況及展望

預估 2026 年家用市場營收金額 45.00 億元， YoY+3.3%

岱宇家用市場營收包括 SOLE 、XTERRA 、FUEL 與 2022 年正式
併入自行車代工廠巨琛。 SOLE 屬中高階品牌，電動跑步機單價
1449 美元、橢圓機單價 1374 美元、健身車單價 899 美元。大眾
市場 XTERRA 與 FUEL， 屬中低價品牌 ， 電動跑步機單價699~899
美元、橢圓機單價 499~999 美元、健身車單價 399~599 美元。

家用市場 2025 年營收金額 43.58 億元， YoY-23.1% 。衰退主因在
於 SOLE 品牌受到美國對等關稅因素，在民眾消費排擠，拖累終
端消費市場疲弱 ， 觀察SOLE 品牌 2025 年營收衰退幅度達 15%，
導致家用市場呈現衰退。

家用市場 1Q26 營收金額 12.69 億元， YoY-4.00% 。觀察 2020 年
疫情帶動家用健身器材需求，經歷 2022~2025 年終端庫存己去化
到相對低檔水準，雖然關稅議題恐影響終端銷費力道，但在各通
路商與品牌商小幅回補庫存 ， 預估2025 年家用健身器材將小幅成
長 2~4% 。宏遠預估 2026 年家用市場營收金額 45.00 億元，
YoY+3.3% 。

預期 2027 年，岱宇家用市場將持續優化產品結構，提供創新產品
與優化服務，包括強化軟體系統以提升用戶黏著度，優化售後服
務與物流效率，以提升獲利率為主。宏遠預估 2027 年家用市場營
收金額 46.00 億元， YoY+2.2% 。

預估 2026 年商用市場營收金額 16.5 億元， YoY+47.3%

商用市場營收主要包括 SPIRIT 為主 ， 另外加上SOLE 部分商用機
種及 UFC 。商用市場 SPIRIT 屬高階品牌，電動跑步機單價 1959
美元、橢圓機單價 2032 美元、健身車單價 1399 美元。

商用市場 2025 年營收金額 11.20 億元， YoY+7.8% 。商用健身器
材營運成長主因在於 SPIRIT 推出內建 Studio 影片的新健身器材
產品，有助商用客戶採用，帶動 SPIRIT 2025 年營收成長 7~8% 。

商用市場 1Q26 營收金額 3.42 億元， YoY+41.0% 。岱宇 2026 年
在商用市場推出重訓產品 ， 己有美國地區型健身房採用 ， 預期2026
年將開始小量出貨。宏遠預估 2026 年商用市場營收金額 16.5 億
元， YoY+47.3% 。

預期 2027 年 ， 岱宇商用市場在新重訓產品在美國地區型健身房持
續出貨外，岱宇也成功打入美國知名連鎖健身房，將有機會放量
成長 ， 宏遠預估2027 年商用市場營收金額 22.4 億元 ，YoY+35.8%。

預估 2026 年稅後盈餘 2.76 億元，由虧轉盈，稅後 EPS 1.60 元

岱宇 2026 年家用市場受惠關稅衝擊淡化 ， 通路商訂單逐漸恢復成
長格局，加上商用市場推出重訓新產品，帶動營收恢復成長，另
外大甲廠處份利益 3Q26 認列，有助岱宇 2026 年營運由虧轉盈。
宏遠預估岱宇 2026 年營收金額 70.00 億元， YoY+14.36% ，預估
營業利益 2.34 億元，虧轉盈，預估稅後盈餘 2.76 億元，虧轉盈，
預估稅後 EPS 1.60 元。

元

訓產品持續放量成長帶動下，岱宇 2027 年本業獲利將呈現成長，

衰退 。 宏遠預估岱宇2027 年營收金額 77.00 億元 ，YoY+10.00% ，

股價相對大盤走勢

所羅門 (2359 TT)  Trading Buy

考量近期機器人題材降溫，評等調整為 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ：雖然機電事業群受惠於半導體擴產、 AI 資料中心衍
生商機釋出，智動化事業群亦已陸續取得諸多實績，並積極進
軍人形機器人領域，長線發展想像空間可期，表現仍符合群益
先前預估，不過，近期機器人題材降溫，所羅門股價受系統性
風險影響程度較大，不利於短線股價表現，故群益將所羅門的
投資建議調整為 Trading Buy 。  
1Q26 稅後淨利 1.34 億元， QoQ-31.24% ：1Q26 營收 8.90 億元，
QoQ-16.74% ，主要係機電事業群 4Q25 認列多起大標案，相關
營收基期較高所致，業外雖認列金融資產評價利益 1.07 億元
(4Q25 為 0.74 億元 )，但匯兌利益低於 4Q25(4Q25 、 1Q26 分別
為 0.91 億元 、0.45 億元 )，1Q26 稅後淨利 1.34 億元 ，QoQ-31.24% 。 
受惠於半導體廠擴產、 AI 資料中心衍生商機，機電事業群 2026
年營收展望佳： 機電事業群營收比重居所羅門各主要事業群之
冠， 2025 年、 1Q26 分別為 40.32% 、 27.15% 。 2026 年迄今機電
事業群接單暢旺，目前在手訂單金額為 20~25 億元 (過去幾年同
期則均約 15 億元 )，營收展望優於 2025 年，原因主要有二， (1)
半導體廠在台灣擴產， (2)CSP 在台灣持續增建 AI 資料中心。  
智動化事業群終端應用涵蓋諸多產業，且積極進軍人形機器人
領域： 智動化事業群營運主軸為以 AI 視覺軟體為基礎，再發展
模組化系統及設備，應用領域不但涵蓋製造、零售、餐飲、倉儲
等傳統產業，更已擴及半導體、面板等高科技產業，總客戶數
已逾 500 家。至於在人形機器人相關領域的部份，所羅門認為，
現階段產業發展重要挑戰為「視覺僅能近距離 (1.5~2 公尺 )辨
識」、「物理資料 (physical data) 學習量龐大、耗時且成本高、
應用不易泛用化」等，故所羅門後續研發方向以「遠距主動視
覺」為主，群益認為，將有助於所羅門強化打入人形機器人供
應鏈的競爭力。

2 群益投顧 
  企業 ESG 資訊揭露：  
在社會層面，所羅門重視員工的職業發展與福利，營造多元共融的工作環
境，並積極參與社會公益活動，集團攜手舊鞋救命協會，號召全員捐贈二手
鞋，幫助非洲赤貧地區學童遠離沙蚤、健康上學；此外，所羅門更邀請社福
團體於集團總部舉辦愛心義賣活動、也捐贈善款予慈善基金會，透過集合眾
人善心，將資源做最有效的利用，致力使社會更加美好。  
在公司治理上，所羅門強化董事會的獨立性與專業性，建立健全的內部控制
與風險管理機制，確保決策過程符合公平性與誠信原則。同時，定期召開董
事會與審計委員會，確保財務報表的準確性與資訊揭露的完整性，讓投資人
能夠充分掌握公司營運狀況。

  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Mediatek (2454.TW)
Potential investment in GUC?
CITI’S TAKE
Digitimes (24-Jun) reported that TSMC may consider selling its holdings in
GUC to Mediatek, which TSMC has denied. Mediatek and GUC have not
yet commented on the matter. In our view, the AI semiconductor supply
chain increasingly rewards companies that can integrate architecture, IP,
manufacturing, packaging, and supply-chain execution. In such a

framework, TSMC would provide advanced node and packaging
technologies; MediaTek would contribute customer relationships, AI
system architecture, networking, and memory optimization, while GUC
would serve as the execution turnkey solution, delivering HBM integration,
chiplet implementation, physical design, and production readiness. If
such a partnership materializes, it could establish a better AI ASIC
ecosystem with a breadth of capabilities in the emerging hyperscale AI

market.
Buy
Catalyst Watch: Upside, expires 30-JUL-26

### Page 2

Mediatek (2454.TW)
24 June 2026 Citi Research
2
MediaTek Is Building an AI ASIC Platform, Not Just Winning TPU Projects
The most important takeaway from MediaTek’s recent Computex event is that the company is positioning itself as a full-stack
AI infrastructure provider rather than just a chip vendor. Management highlighted a portfolio spanning IoT, automotive, AI PCs,
custom ASICs, NVIDIA GB10, DGX Spark, and NVLink Fusion-based infrastructure. For AI ASIC, MediaTek is growing

investments in high speed SerDes, custom memory, chiplet architectures, and AI networking fabrics. While MediaTek has
accumulated substantial expertise in architecture, system integration, and customer engagement through Google, NVIDIA, and
automotive programs, GUC possesses a different set of capabilities. GUC’s portfolio includes HBM controllers and PHYs, UCIe
chiplet interfaces, GLink die-to-die interconnect technologies, CoWoS and SoIC implementation expertise, and advanced

2.5D/ packaging solutions. More importantly, GUC has developed a comprehensive turnkey model that spans specification,
physical implementation, advanced packaging, manufacturing enablement, and production support. In essence, MediaTek
knows how to architect complex AI systems, while GUC specializes in turning those architectures into manufacturable products
at advanced nodes.
The Real Prize Is HBM, Chiplets, and Advanced Packaging
In our view, the strongest strategic overlap between the two companies lies in AI packaging and memory technologies.

MediaTek is working on custom SRAM, LPDDR, Compute-in-Memory, and Custom HBM as key future differentiators. At the
same time, GUC has emerged as providers of chiplet-related IP. Over the past two years, GUC has introduced HBM3 and HBM4
controller and PHY solutions, UCIe chiplet interfaces, and complete 2.5D/3D ASIC services built around CoWoS and SoIC. As
AI accelerators evolve from monolithic designs to chiplet-based architectures connected through HBM and advanced

packaging, GUC’s expertise could significantly shorten MediaTek’s learning curve and reduce time-to-market for future CSP
programs.
Long-term AI upside intact; near term non-AI business also better than expected; Reiterate Buy
We are bullish on Mediatek’s long-term growth outlook in AI ASIC, yet we also note that its non-AI business is also showing
better than expected momentum. Due to broad-based structural cost increase across the semiconductor supply chain,

Mediatek will start to revise its pricing across all products. We believe the surge in AI-driven computing demand is accelerating
technology upgrades across the industry, making component shortages and price increases the new normal. And we see
potentially better-than-expected near-term revenue and GM for Mediatek in 2Q and 3Q. We reiterate our Buy on the company.

### Page 4

Mediatek (2454.TW)
24 June 2026 Citi Research
4
Mediatek
Valuation
Our NT$5,950 target price for MediaTek is based on 36x P/E to 2027/2028 EPS, which is the high end of its five-year average
forward P/E given the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We believe our target PE is also justified
by Mediatek’s global and regional peers’ trading at an average of 36x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should provide more
upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek well prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite

lukewarm smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G migration in emerging markets and its
Dimensity series should also gain shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing stabilizing TV and IoT
demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from

AI ASIC business; 2) more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall demand related to macroeconomic
risks, especially for smartphone demand; and 4) further price hikes from foundries.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Global Server: Raising both AI and General servers; better mix and
higher memory costs drive CSP spending
24 June 2026 | 1:13AM HKT
We raise our global servers TAM to reflect the ramp-up of AI server racks, and
higher memory costs driving CSP spending. We refresh our forecasts for (1) AI

server racks (NVL72-equivalent), (2) AI servers (8-GPU equivalent), (3) General
servers, (4) AI servers implied GPU vs. ASIC, and (5) leading US and China cloud
capex. We expect AI server rack (NVL72-equivalent) shipment (incl. Nvidia and
AMD) to reach 55k/ 105k/ 163k in 2026-28E, increasing by 16% / 20% in 2027 /
28E, reflec ting our positive view on AI server racks, which carry more intense design
and stronger GPU interconnection. On ASIC AI servers, we expect growing ASIC

adoption, with ASICs a ccounting for 50%/ 52%/ 55% of AI chips in 2026-28E (vs. our
previous estimates of 43% / 50% / 55% in 2026-28E). Our global AI server volume
forecast implies demand for AI chips at 19m/ 27m/ 32m in 2026-28E, which
increases from our last update due to our revised outlook on AI server racks
(NVL72-equivalent) and AI servers (8-GPU equivalent) across ASIC and GPU base.
We forecast a positive demand trend for GPU and ASIC platforms, and new AI servers

based on new networking architectures from 2027 which we expect will further
enhance the advancement in computing performance. We expect the continuing AI
infrastructure cycle to support growth through 2028E.
AI servers: Buy Wiwynn / Wistron (ODM), Hon Hai / FII (ODM), LandMark (Silicon
photonics), VPEC (Silicon photonics), AVC / Fositek (liquid cooling), Auras (liquid
cooling), King Slide (rail kits), Chenbro (chassis), EMC (CCL), GCE (PCB), Eoptolink

(Optical connections), TSMC (foundry; on CL), MPI (probe cards), WinWay (sockets),
Aspeed (fabless), and Hon Precision (FT handler).

should be aware that the firm ma y have a conflict of interest that could affect the objectivity of this report.

Servers: AI server racks +190%/+89%/+56% YoY in 2026-28E; General server volume +14%/ +6%/
+6% YoY in 2026-28E
Server TAM estimate revisions
AI server racks: We raise our forecast for AI server racks powered by NVIDIA to 92k /
148k in 2027 / 28E, with our estimates being raised by 19% / 22% in 2027 / 28E, while
keeping AI server racks powered by AMD largely unchanged at 5k / 13k / 15k in
2026-28E. With continuous specifica
tion upgrade, we expect the dollar content per AI

server rack will continue to grow. Overall, we expect the AI server rack global value TAM
to grow at 118% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$561bn, or 51% of the global server value TAM
by 2028E. By vendor, we continue to expect global leading CSPs to be the major
customers, with leading ODM direct players as the major suppliers. We expect Hon Hai /
FII to maintain the leading market position among ODMs and with continuous
specifica tion upgrade driving its global market share from 55% in 2026E to 69% by

2028E.

### Page 4

AI servers: We raise AI server (8-GPU) equivalent shipment by 18% / 24% / 11% in
2026-28E to 1.9m / 2.4m / 2.6m in 2026-28E, mainly driven by rising ASIC-based AI
server demand. The custom-design provides ASIC-based AI servers optimized for AI
computation with better efficienc
y, such as lower latency, or memory savings via
reduced expensive read/write cycles. We continue to see new generation ASICs
launched by global leading CSPs, supporting AI server growth: we expect the AI servers

(8-GPU) global value TAM to grow at 26% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$295bn, or 27% of
global server value TAM by 2028E.
General servers: We raise general server revenue growth from +15% / +6% / +6% YoY in
2026-28E to +28% / +17% / +11% YoY in 2026-28E driven by high memory costs and
ongoing specifica
tion upgrade driving AI workloads. Intel launches Intel Xeon 6+
processors in Computex 2026 (link) to facilitate Agentic AI: CPU servers are served as the

control plane, driving system-level performance and efficienc y under rising agentic AI
workloads, while supporting data movement and sustained inferencing. We expect
general servers’ global value TAM to grow at 18% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$245bn, or
22% of global server value TAM by 2028E.

### Page 5

CSP capex: Regarding customer spending, our US internet team forecasts that leading
US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets by 76%/
35%/ 8% YoY in 2026-28E, with our estimates being raised by 8% / 27% / 25% in
2026-28E to US$1,145bn by 2028E. Within the China internet space, we expect leading
Chinese cloud platforms to grow capex by 80%/ 20%/ 18% YoY in 2026-28E, with our
estimates being raised by 37% / 44% / 55% in 2026-28E. The strong increase in CSP

capex reflec
ts the high memory costs and growing demand on AI workloads. In terms of
capex structure, we estimate chipsets (GPU, ASIC, CPU) remains the major contributors,
along with memory (HBM), and followed by networking and other components (e.g.
cooling, power, chassis, rail kits, etc.). Compared to global CSPs, we estimate China CSP
spending on chipsets would be more diversified a
cross GPU and ASICs considering
various local fabless suppliers and customers’ varying demand on AI scenarios / tasks.

, Amazon, Meta, Alphabet, Oracle, Baba, Tencent, Baidu.

GS Factor Profile
The Goldman Sachs Factor Profile provides investment context for a stock by comparing key attributes to the market (i.e. our universe of rated stocks)
and its sector peers. The four key attributes depicted are: Growth, Financial Returns, Multiple (e.g. valuation) and Integrated (a c omposite of Growth,
Financial Returns and Multiple). Growth, Financial Returns and Multiple are calculated by using normalized ranks for specific metrics f or each stock. The

normalized ranks for the metrics are then averaged and converted into percentiles for the relevant attribute. The precise calcula tion of each metric may
vary depending on the fiscal y ear, industry and region, but the standard approach is as follows:
Growth is based on a stock’s forward-looking sales growth, EBITDA growth and EPS growth (for financial s tocks, only EPS and sales growth), with a
higher percentile indicating a higher growth company. Financial Returns is based on a stock’s forward-looking ROE, ROCE and CROCI (for financial

stocks, only ROE), with a higher percentile indicating a company with higher financial r eturns. Multiple is based on a stock’s forward-looking P/E, P/B,
price/dividend (P/D), EV/EBITDA, EV/FCF and EV/Debt Adjusted Cash Flow (DACF) (for financial s tocks, only P/E, P/B and P/D), with a higher percentile
indicating a stock trading at a higher multiple. The Integrated percentile is calculated as the average of the Growth percentile, Financial Returns

percentile and (100% - Multiple percentile).
Financial Returns and Multiple use the Goldman Sachs analyst forecasts at the fiscal y ear-end at least three quarters in the future. Growth uses inputs
for the fiscal y ear at least seven quarters in the future compared with the year at least three quarters in the future (on a per-share basis for all metrics).
For a more detailed description of how we calculate the GS Factor Profile, please c ontact your GS representative.

M&A Rank
Across our global coverage, we examine stocks using an M&A framework, considering both qualitative factors and quantitative factors (which may vary
across sectors and regions) to incorporate the potential that certain companies could be acquired. We then assign a M&A rank as a means of sc oring
companies under our rated coverage from 1 to 3, with 1 representing high (30%-50%) probability of the company becoming an acquisition targ et, 2
representing medium (15%-30%) probability and 3 representing low (0%-15%) probability. For companies ranked 1 or 2, in line with our s tandard

departmental guidelines we incorporate an M&A component into our target price. M&A rank of 3 is considered immaterial and theref ore does not
factor into our price target, and may or may not be discussed in research.
Quantum
Quantum is Goldman Sachs’ proprietary database providing access to detailed financial statement histories, forecasts and ratios. It can be used for
in-depth analysis of a single company, or to make comparisons between companies in differ ent sectors and markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a B uy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not C overed), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region ’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the r eturn across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment R eview Committee or

other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such s tocks.
Total return potential represents the upside or downside differ ential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all cov ered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership .

Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at
https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are leg al, regulatory or

policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment
rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical de vice that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient f undamental basis for determining an

investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effec t for this stock and should not be relied
upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

believe this vertical integration strategy positions Accton to capture a larger 
share of the datacenter value chain as cloud architectures evolve.

Initiate at OW with Jun-27 PT of NT$3,600 
We initiate coverage of Accton with an OW rating and Jun-27 PT of NT$3,600 (around 
~26x 12M fwd EPS, at the higher end of mid-cycle valuations). We are positive on 
Accton due to 1) resumed AWS ASIC momentum in 2H26; 2) 800G/1.6T datacenter 
switch upgrades with a multi-year AI-driven accelerating upgrade trend; 3) new 800G 
switch project ramp; and 4) AI networking TAM expansion on rising Ethernet adoption 
in the AI scale-up fabric. Consequently, we forecast 44%/51% revenue/earnings CAGRs 
over 2025-28.

Key downside risks include: 1) faster-than-expected market share loss in AWS AI ASIC 
module; 2) slower-than-expected 800G/1.6T networking upgrade trend; 3) weaker-than-
expected AWS ASIC demand; and 4) larger-than-expected margin dilution during 
vertical integration.

JPMe above consensus estimates on accelerating datacenter 
switch upgrade cycle and positive view on AWS Trn 3 
momentum 
We believe consensus is underestimating both (1) AI ASIC demand and (2) 
the 800G/1.6T data center switch refresh cycle. Our industry research suggests AWS’s 
AI ASIC momentum is likely to remain strong into next year. In data center switching, 
we expect the 800G upgrade cycle to accelerate across leading hyperscalers beginning 
in 2H25, with an initial ramp of 1.6T in 2H26, supported by Nvidia’s 800G/1.6T 
networking adoption roadmap.

We expect the super general server upcycle and secular AI server demand to drive the 
traditional datacenter Ethernet ODM switch demand in the next few years. In addition to 
the traditional Ethernet fabric, AI compute fabric will create an incremental switch TAM 
for Accton as CSPs rapidly increase Ethernet adoption.

AI compute fabric will accelerate the 800G/1.6T upgrade cycle, which could even 
become a bigger market vs. the traditional one, on our estimates. Our research also 
indicates Accton has secured a new 800G program at a key U.S. hyperscaler, which 
implies that Accton will even outgrow the fast-growing market. There could be potential 
upside for Accton’s networking biz from increased penetration in scale-up switching at a 
key ASIC customer.

Overall, the combination of strong top-line momentum and operating leverage drives 
our 2026/27 earnings estimates to be ~12%/6% above consensus estimates.

Overview of historical stock price and stock catalysts 
Revenue momentum has been the key driver of Accton’s share price. Since listing in 
1995, Accton’s revenue has increased by more than 50x, while GM has generally 
remained within the 10–20% range. Over the same period, the stock has appreciated by 
more than 90x.

Accton traded in a prolonged range during 2003–2014, reflecting cyclical networking 
demand. In 2H15, revenue momentum began to accelerate as the company became a 
JDM (Joint Development Manufacturer) partner to key CSP (Cloud Service Provider) 
customers, enabling it to break into the data center white-box switch market.

Revenue growth re-accelerated to a 22% CAGR in 2015–2019 (vs. 2% in 2002–2015), 
and GM recovered from a trough of 15% in 2014 to ~20% in 2019. We attribute this 
inflection to the structural adoption of data center white-box switches. Notably, 
Accton’s stock rose 17.5x over this period (vs. +52% for TAIEX).

Momentum paused in 2020–1H23 due to the networking chip shortage and a mix shift 
in Meta’s 400G switch program, but re-accelerated in 2H23 on strong Amazon AI ASIC 
demand and rising expectations for AI-driven networking upgrades over the medium to 
longer term.

The stock corrected in 9M24 amid a networking destocking cycle and Gaudi demand 
downside, but reached a new high in 4Q24 on stronger-than-expected Amazon ASIC 
demand and an improving networking upgrade outlook.

In April 2025, shares sold off on rising macro uncertainty tied to Trump’s trade policy, 
before a V-shaped rebound to new highs supported by tariff exemptions and robust AWS 
AI ASIC and networking demand.

Shares traded range-bound in 2H25 on concerns around the AWS transition, but have 
continued to break records since late 2025 driven by better-than-expected revenue 
momentum, aided by a smoother-than-expected AWS ASIC transition, potential AWS 
ASIC demand upside, and a new 800G customer ramp.

Looking ahead, we expect more sustained stock momentum into next year on resilient 
AWS AI ASIC demand and 800G/1.6T switch upgrade cycles.

to sustain a premium P/E 
Accton’s valuation has re-rated meaningfully (from 5–10x in 2015 to 20–30x today), as 
it gained exposure to the data center white-box switch market. The multiple peaked in 
1H21 at over +2x standard deviation (or higher) on anticipation of the 400G cycle ramp, 
before de-rating as the company lost share to a key U.S. competitor in the 400G 
generation.

Looking forward, we expect earnings momentum to further accelerate with potential 
earnings upside in the coming months. We forecast a ~50% earnings CAGR in 2025-28

We expect Accton to outgrow its major peers (JPMe 50%+ earnings CAGR in 2025-27 
vs. industry median of 25-30%), driven by an accelerating datacenter whitebox switch 
upgrade cycle, market share gains in a key U.S. CSP 800G project, and secular ASIC 
demand. Therefore, we expect Accton stock to continue trading at a premium multiple 
versus peers averaging at ~25-30x.

In addition, the stocks of Accton’s key industry peers, Celestica and Arista, have traded 
at a 20-30% premium despite slower earnings momentum. There could be a potential re-
rating opportunity for Accton to the 25-30x range, supported by stronger earnings power 
and its differentiated vertical integration—from networking cards, data center switch 
systems, and optical components, to AI chip modules and potentially server systems.

Accton also screens with the highest ROE among peers, reflecting efficient capital 
deployment and rapid earnings growth. Overall, our Jun-27 PT of NT$3,600 is based on 
~26x 12M fwd EPS and implies a valuation above mid-cycle.

I. Network switches - the next key driver for 
Accton 
We see network switches as the next key growth driver for Accton. Switch revenue 
growth has accelerated over the past 4–6 quarters, led by the ramp in high-end platforms 
(400G/800G). Demand for high-end data center switches is being pulled forward by AI 
—both the build-out of AI networking fabrics and the incremental expansion of existing 
Ethernet networks as AI-inference-driven traditional/ASIC server deployments scale 
(we estimate data center switching demand at ~15–20% CAGR in 2020–2024).

Our industry research also suggests Accton has been gaining m/s in Meta’s 800G 
program since late 2025. Management indicated that 800G revenue has reached low-
teens % of total revenue in 1Q26 (up from high single digits in 4Q25), driven by a new 
800G customer ramp, and we expect momentum to build further over the next few 
quarters. We believe a multi-year server upcycle and an accelerating switch upgrade 
cycle should sustain strong growth in Accton’s switch business. Overall, we forecast 
Accton to outgrow the white-box switch market, with a 2025–28 switch revenue CAGR 
of ~90% versus an industry TAM CAGR of ~75%, driven by continued market share 
gains.

Of note, our TAM does not include scale-up fabrics, AMD’s GPU related switch TAM, 
and AI ASIC switch TAM other than AWS. There could also be upside to our TAM 
estimates from enriching Nvidia’s product offering (i.e. Nvidia’s “extreme co-design” 
Rubin platform). LPX/CMX/Vera CPU standalone racks will likely create additional 
ToR switch demand.

switch demand 
Leading hyperscalers have been stepping up data center investment since 2H23, with AI

aggregate cloud capex for the top four U.S. hyperscalers to +80% YoY growth in 2026

Data center infrastructure spending extends well beyond servers to include networking, 
data center shells, utilities, and other components. While switches represent only ~5-
10% of total data center hardware spend, they are critical to connectivity, security, and 
overall data center efficiency. As AI compute scales and cluster sizes expand, bandwidth 
requirements and switch capacity needs rise sharply to support higher-throughput data 
transmission.

Over the past 2–3 years, CSPs have prioritized AI server investment and data center 
build-outs, while networking has lagged. Gartner® data shows ODM-direct server 
spending increased 207%/127% in 2024/2025, versus ODM-direct switch spending 
growth of 18%/103% over the same period. We expect CSPs to step up networking 
spending to reduce latency and improve data transmission rates. Overall, we believe 
aggressive AI server investment will pull forward a multi-year networking switch 
upgrade cycle—supportive for white-box switch vendors such as Accton.

AI-driven general-purpose server spending drives demand for 
generic networking fabric investments 
• We introduce our proprietary, bottom-up analysis of Accton’s generic white-box 
Ethernet switch TAM. Given Accton’s focus on ToR switches for U.S. hyperscalers, 
our framework centers on projected demand for generic white-box ToR switches at 
the top 4 U.S. CSPs. We also include the front-end AI server network in our 
forecast. Overall, we forecast a ~43% CAGR over 2025–28 for the general-purpose 
white-box Ethernet ToR switch revenue TAM, driven by (1) stronger general-
purpose server demand, (2) rapid build-out of AI front-end network fabrics, and (3) 
ASP uplift from ongoing speed migration. Key assumptions include: 
• Top 4 U.S. CSP server shipments: Historical server motherboard shipments are 
based on ODM feedback. We expect shipments to accelerate over the next few years 
on AI-driven inference demand, and estimate shipment growth of 40%/25%/20% in 
2026/27/28. There could be upside risks to our general server shipment assumptions 
driven by fast-growing agentic AI demand. 
• NICs per server: General-purpose servers typically have 2–4 NICs. We expect 
NICs per server to gradually trend higher as bandwidth requirements increase with 
AI inference workloads. 
• Ports per NIC: NICs typically carry 1–2 ports. We assume 2 ports per NIC in our 
analysis. 
• Oversubscription ratio: In a leaf-spine topology, ToR switches connect to server-
facing NIC ports (downlinks) and to spine switches (uplinks). In traditional IT 
spine-leaf fabrics, the downlink-to-uplink bandwidth ratio is typically below 3:1. We 
expect this ratio to compress over time as AI inference workloads increase and 
uplink bandwidth requirements rise. 
• White-box switch penetration: White-box Ethernet adoption in data center ToR 
switches has been increasing, supported by better TCO (Total Cost of Ownership) 
and a more mature open NOS (Network Operating Software) ecosystem. 
• Port shipment mix by speed: While 400G/800G ports are being adopted in AI 
server front-end networks (initially in 2023/2026, respectively), 100G should remain 
the mainstream choice for the broader, generic networking fabric.

• ASP per port by speed: Our port ASP assumptions are anchored in our 650 Group 
historical data and our U.S. team forecasts. While we expect price erosion at a given 
speed, we forecast a 6% CAGR in blended ASPs over 2025–28, driven by a faster 
upgrade cycle.

AI/ML drives both front-end and back-end networking 
upgrades 
AI data centers typically run two distinct networks: a front-end network (traditional 
Ethernet fabric that connects to the external network/Internet) and a back-end network 
(cluster fabric that connects the AI training pods) (see Figure 12, illustration of front-
end and back-end networks in AI/ML clusters).

We believe AI is creating an incremental back-end GPU training network while also 
pulling forward refresh cycles in the front-end. We expect back-end to upgrade faster, 
reflecting the roughly annual cadence of the GPU platform cycle. At the same time, we

12

### Page 13

Albert Hung AC 
(886-2) 2725-9875 
albert.hung@jpmchase.com 
Asia Pacific Equity Research 
25 June 2026

expect ongoing upgrades in the traditional front-end network as training and inference 
drive materially higher bandwidth requirements and overall data volumes.

Arista management has suggested that every dollar of back-end network spend by data 
center customers can drive an additional dollar of similar spend on the front-end 
network—implying that the front-end also scales with the growing size of AI clusters. 
We also see continued spec upgrades in front-end NICs (e.g., BlueField 3 400G in 
GB200/300 moving to BlueField 4 800G in VR200 systems). Our research indicates 
that one key U.S. hyperscaler has also upgraded its in-house front-end NIC solution for 
NVL72 servers from 200G in GB200/300 to 400G in VR200.

Beyond AI servers, we are also seeing a multi-year general-purpose server upcycle 
driven by rising agentic-AI workloads, which should further support traditional front-
end networking demand.

Ethernet networking vendors (e.g., Arista, Juniper, Cisco, Accton) have served the front-
end networking market for decades and should benefit from the AI-driven upgrade cycle 
(particularly white-box data center switch vendors). While AI GPU back-end networks 
have historically skewed toward InfiniBand, we see Ethernet increasingly penetrating 
AI clusters, creating incremental TAM for Ethernet switch vendors. Our research 
suggests that several of the top four U.S. hyperscalers (e.g., Amazon, Google) are 
inclined to deploy Ethernet in their back-end networks as well.

Figure 12: Illustration of the front-end and back-end networks in AI/ML clusters

Source: Cisco.

There are two types of GPU networks: AI scale-up and AI scale-out. Today, Accton is 
primarily exposed to GPU scale-out networks through a key CSP customer, and we 
believe it is gaining share at an additional U.S. hyperscaler. While Nvidia’s GPU servers 
are likely to remain anchored to proprietary NVLink for scale-up connectivity, we 
believe AMD platforms and CSP in-house ASIC servers are more open to non-Nvidia 
scale-up options, including Ethernet-based approaches (e.g., ESUN/SUE), UALink, and

13

PCIe-based solutions.

We are also seeing early signs of Ethernet-based scale-up network development, notably 
OCP’s ESUN and Broadcom’s SUE (Scale-up Ethernet). If Ethernet-based scale-up 
switching is adopted in next-generation AI server architectures, it could open a 
meaningful incremental TAM opportunity for Accton. Our research indicates Accton is 
already engaged with a key ASIC customer on scale-up switch design work.

Smart Network Interface Card (Smart 
NIC) 
A smart NIC is a programmable 
accelerator equipped with computing 
power that boosts datacenter networking, 
security, and storage functions by 
offloading non-compute centric tasks 
from the CPU. There are different types 
of smart NICs such as ASIC, FPGA, and 
SoC. 
Higher GPU bandwidth drives back-end scale-out switch demand 
A network interface card (NIC) connects an end device (e.g., storage, GPU, and CPU 
servers) to the compute network fabric. NIC-to-switch link speed is a key trigger for 
upgrades across the switching stack—from ToR/leaf to spine. In practice, datacenter 
operators aim to align NIC/switch and switch/switch speeds at defined “match points” to 
optimize throughput, latency, and overall network efficiency. Upgrade timing is also 
shaped by total cost of ownership and ecosystem readiness (e.g., availability and 
maturity of switch ICs, SerDes, and optical transceivers). When speeds are mismatched, 
networks can experience higher latency, inefficient utilization, and ultimately 
suboptimal performance.

NIC specifications also influence protocol choices (e.g., InfiniBand vs. Ethernet). 
Nvidia’s ConnectX SuperNICs support both Ethernet and InfiniBand, while most CSP 
in-house networking solutions are Ethernet-based rather than InfiniBand.

Table 4: Key components of a datacenter leaf-spine networking system

Networking equipment/devices Functions  
Network interface card (NIC) Gateways between server nodes and networks  
Leaf switch/top-of-rack switch First connections from NICs to the rest of networks 
Spine switch Responsible for the east-west traffic. The port capacity of spine switches determines the scale/number of racks 
Connects different devices in networks. There are copper or optical cables. The former has cheaper cost and consumes less 
Cable power, while the latter could connects the devices with better connecting efficiency in long distance. Usually copper cable is 
used within rack, while optical cable is used between racks.  
Optical transceiver Allows longer connecting distance between leaf-to-spine switches

Source: Company data. 
As accelerator chips become more compute-intensive and memory bandwidth increases, 
we expect NIC specifications to step up (e.g., higher link speeds and more advanced 
feature sets) and/or NIC volumes per server to rise. For example, Nvidia’s A100/H100/ 
H200 platforms have moved to 400G SmartNICs (ConnectX-7), versus 100G SmartNIC 
configurations used in P100/V100-era deployments circa 2016.

While initial Blackwell server configurations continued to use 400G ConnectX-7 (CX7) 
NICs as networking adapters, largely reflecting time-to-market priorities, Nvidia’s 800G 
SmartNIC (ConnectX-8; CX8) reached general availability in 2H25 and GB300 systems 
have shifted to CX8. In our view, this transition—alongside a smoother GB200-to-
GB300 ramp driven by the board redesign and strong GB300 demand this year—should 
expedite the 800G switch upgrade cycle.

Looking ahead, we expect Nvidia and AMD to maintain a short GPU roadmap cadence 
to meet surging AI compute demand—an important catalyst for continued networking 
switch upgrades. Nvidia’s next-generation GPUs (i.e., Vera Rubin servers) are expected 
to adopt 1.6T-class NICs (e.g., ConnectX-9). In addition, our research suggests that the 
NIC-to-GPU ratio doubles in VR200 systems to 2:1 (vs. 1:1 in GB200/300), with 
potential for a further step-up in the Vera Rubin Ultra.

A faster switch upgrade cycle—paired with the exponential growth in GPU memory 
bandwidth—supports a materially expanding AI switch TAM. We believe Accton is 
well positioned to benefit from the sustained secular demand in GPU scale-out 
networks.

In the analysis below, we size the AI-related high-speed switch TAM and assess the 
associated growth opportunity for Accton. Given Nvidia’s dominant position in the AI 
data center GPU market, we anchor our framework on Nvidia-led AI infrastructure and 
estimate port demand based on our Nvidia GPU system forecasts and bandwidth

GB200-class platforms to 800G in Blackwell/Rubin HGX/GB300. Our research further 
suggests that GPU bandwidth could double to 1.6T in VR200 and then double again in

For ASP, our 800G switch port assumptions are based on our BoM cost estimates (see 
Table 9). On this basis, we estimate Nvidia’s high-speed ToR switch revenue TAM for 
the back-end GPU network at ~US$2.6bn in 2025. We expect this TAM to expand at a 
126% CAGR over 2025–28, driven by the step-function increase in GPU bandwidth and 
sustained AI demand.

We see even stronger growth potential for Nvidia’s GPU networking Ethernet switch 
TAM as Ethernet continues to gain share versus InfiniBand. Dell’Oro estimates Ethernet 
penetration in AI back-end networks increased from ~20% in 2023 to 60%+ by the end 
of 2025. We expect Ethernet adoption to continue rising over the next few years within 
Nvidia’s GPU back-end networks, and forecast a ~164% CAGR for the Ethernet switch 
TAM over 2025–28.

Accelerating network upgrade cycles driven by AI compute 
Gartner® data points to a rapid pace of speed migration in data center switching, driven 
by surging data generation and rising workload complexity—particularly with the 
emergence of gen-AI—while enterprise customers continue to upgrade at a much slower 
pace. Mainstream enterprise switch ports remain anchored at 10G (90%+ of campus 
switch port shipments in 2025). In contrast, for datacenter customers, sub-10G ports 
have fallen to ~15% of total switch port shipments from 90%+ in 2015, while ports 
above 100G have climbed to 50%+ versus near-zero in 2015.

Data center customers kicked off the 100G upgrade cycle in 2015, and the transition 
from 100G to 200/400G took roughly seven years. By comparison, the 400G-to-800G 
migration window has compressed to ~3–4 years (2022–25). Our industry research 
suggests the initial 1.6T ramp is likely to begin in 2H26, implying the 800G-to-1.6T 
transition could compress to less than two years.

A s
hortening upgrade cadence is supportive of data center switch ASP expansion. We 
see Accton as a key beneficiary, given its strong positioning in high-speed white-box 
data center switches.

Continued ASP expansion in datacenter switches on content growth and a shorter 
upgrade cadence 
We estimate an 800G bare-metal switch costs a little more than 2x a 400G switch, 
primarily driven by higher content in switch silicon and upgrades in PCB/CCL materials 
required for higher-speed transmission. Looking ahead, 1.6T platforms are expected to 
adopt Tomahawk 6, with CCL material upgrading to M9—providing a continued 
tailwind to ASP expansion. This underscores meaningful revenue upside for Accton as

18

### Page 19

Albert Hung AC 
(886-2) 2725-9875 
albert.hung@jpmchase.com 
Asia Pacific Equity Research 
25 June 2026

800G/1.6T penetration rises.

Speed migration remains the core driver of data center switch ASP expansion. Each 
generational step-up requires more advanced components—switch ICs, CCL/PCBs, 
power delivery, and thermal systems—pushing BoM costs higher. For example, our 
industry research suggests Tomahawk 5 ASP is ~2–3x that of Tomahawk 4.

At the board level, higher speeds typically require both an upgrade in CCL materials and 
a higher PCB layer count to maintain signal integrity. Our research suggests CCL costs 
step up materially in 800G switches, where materials move to M8 versus M6+ in 400G 
designs. Combined with higher layer counts, this can drive a multi-fold increase in 
PCB-related costs, reinforcing the upward trajectory in system ASPs.

Finally, we see incremental upside from broader adoption of liquid cooling in high-end 
switches as switch silicon and optical components become more power-dense and 
hyperscalers increasingly optimize for data center energy efficiency. CPO adoption 
could also significantly increase the switch BoM and bodes well for ODM margins 
given the high complexity, in our view. Our research also indicates that Nvidia’s 
Spectrum-X switch chip has a higher price point vs. Broadcom's Tomahawk solution.

Given the wide variation in switch radix specifications, we focus on ASP per switch port 
in the discussion below. In a new cycle, switching vendors typically capture a 
meaningful ASP premium in the early ramp, followed by ~5% annual ASP erosion as 
volumes scale and the technology matures. As a result, the pace of speed migration—
and the resulting port shipment mix—are the key swing factors for the blended ASP 
trajectory.

For example, while we saw a low-single-digit annual decline in ASP for each individual 
data center switch port speed over 2018–25, the mix shift toward higher-speed ports still 
drove blended ASP growth of ~4% CAGR. In contrast, Gartner® data show that the 
blended ASP for campus switch ports declined by ~1% per year over the same period. 
We attribute this gap to slower speed migration on campus switches, which limits mix-
driven ASP uplift.

Lion's share in Amazon and Meta’s 100G cycle 
Accton has long been a bellwether in the white-box switching ecosystem. In 2015, HP 
collaborated with Accton to develop “BriteBox” switches targeted at 10G/40G 
configurations for CSP customers. In parallel, as a key contributor to Meta’s Open 
Compute Project (OCP) switching efforts, Accton’s wholly owned subsidiary, Edgecore, 
launched the industry’s first open network switch (Wedge-16X) based on Meta’s Wedge 
design and running Facebook Open Switch System (FBOSS). Shipments of Accton’s 
Wedge and BriteBox platforms began in 1H15.

Supported by first-mover advantage, sustained R&D investment, and deep hyperscaler 
relationships, Accton established a dominant position in the white-box data center 
market (50%+ share) during the 100G cycle (2015–21), including an estimated lion’s 
share (80%+) of Amazon and Meta’s 100G switch volumes. In contrast, Google 
primarily outsources switch manufacturing to EMS vendors (CLS); Accton has 
historically opted not to pursue Google’s switch supply chain given less attractive 
margin economics.

M/s shift in Meta’s 200G/400G cycle 
Meta introduced Minipack in March 2019, a 32-port 400G fabric switch built by 
Edgecore Networks. It was the industry’s first 400G open network switch. Minipack

22

### Page 23

Albert Hung AC 
(886-2) 2725-9875 
albert.hung@jpmchase.com 
Asia Pacific Equity Research 
25 June 2026

delivers materially better efficiency versus its predecessor, Backpack, using roughly half 
the power and space, and runs on Meta’s FBOSS software.

That said, 400G did not move into a true volume cycle until 2021–22, when Meta 
launched Minipack 2 (e.g., Wedge 400/400C) and selected Celestica as an ODM partner. 
We attribute the slower-than-expected 400G ramp to limited availability of high-
volume, low-cost 400G optics, early technical friction, and COVID-related disruptions. 
As the 400G program scaled from 2022, these factors contributed to a market share shift 
within Meta from Accton to Celestica.

Clawing back market share in Meta’s 800G cycle in 2026 
Entering the 800G cycle, we expect Accton to retain its leadership position in Amazon’s 
800G deployments while also regaining share within Meta’s 800G program. At OCP 
2024, Meta introduced Minipack 3 / Cisco 8501 800G switches, based on Broadcom 
Tomahawk 5 and Cisco Silicon One G200, and manufactured by Celestica and Wistron, 
respectively.

Meta subsequently announced another OCP 800G platform, Minipack 3N, in October 
2025. The design is based on Nvidia’s Spectrum-X Ethernet switch IC and is positioned 
as a key building block for Meta’s next-generation AI data center infrastructure. Meta 
has selected Accton as the manufacturing partner for this program. Our industry 
research suggests Minipack 3N began an initial ramp in 4Q25, with demand 
strengthening this year—supportive of Accton’s switch revenue momentum.

Potential content upside from CPO switch adoption at the ToR 
layer 
Accton/Edgecore announced a scale-out CPO switch design based on Broadcom’s 
Tomahawk 6 last year. We are seeing rising traction for CPO in AI networking as 
customers push for higher bandwidth density, lower latency, and better power efficiency. 
Nvidia is currently the market leader in CPO switching, but we expect Broadcom to 
narrow the gap as Tomahawk 6 CPO configurations ramp.

For switch ODMs such as Accton, CPO should translate into higher MVA per system 
given greater platform complexity and a higher likelihood of liquid cooling adoption. 
More importantly, CPO architectures integrate optical capabilities (optical engines) 
directly into the switch platform, versus traditional electrical switches that rely on 
pluggable optical transceivers. This shifts a meaningful amount of optical content into 
the switch itself—often a higher-value portion of the bill of materials than the base 
switch system—creating material ASP upside. CPO platforms typically incorporate 
optical engines, fiber alignment units, external laser sources, and associated fiber/cable/ 
connectors.

Overall, we view rising CPO adoption as a positive for Accton, supported by its design 
execution track record, vertical integration capability, and, in our view, improving 
margin mix.

II. Sustained growth momentum of AWS 
ASIC business 
We expect Accton’s AWS ASIC-related revenue to continue growing over the next few 
years, as robust AWS AI demand and incremental content wins more than offset 
headwinds from (1) an m/s shift in the OAM/UBB business and (2) board design 
changes in the Trainium 3 generation.

24

We continue to see solid AWS AI ASIC demand, and view Trainium as a key enabler 
within Amazon’s AI stack. We forecast a ~20% CAGR in Trainium ASIC shipments 
over 2026–28. In addition, our industry research suggests Accton has secured the scale-
up switch tray assembly scope within the Trainium 3 program. We also see incremental 
content opportunities in Trainium 3 boards, including PCIe switches and potential cold 
plates.

Overall, we forecast 17%/30% revenue growth for Accton’s Network Application 
segment in 2026/27. A key downside risk is further delay to the Trn3 project.

Resumed AWS ASIC momentum in 2Q26; demand signals 
turning more constructive 
Accton’s Network Application Equipment (NAE) revenue is now ~10x larger than in 
2017, and the segment’s revenue exposure has expanded to ~62% in 2025 from 12% in 
2017. We attribute this step-change primarily to strong AWS ASIC demand and 
Accton’s leading position in the OAM/UBB ODM business.

AWS ASIC-related revenue momentum has been muted over the past two quarters, 
largely reflecting project transitions. Looking ahead, we expect momentum to re-
accelerate from late 2Q26 as AWS T3 ramp. Importantly, we have seen incremental 
improvement in recent demand indicators, which reinforces our view that AWS AI 
ASIC demand remains on an upward trajectory into 2026/27.

In Amazon’s recent annual shareholder letter, the CEO highlighted an ambition to scale 
the revenue run-rate of its in-house silicon portfolio (including Trainium, Graviton, and 
Nitro) to US$50bn from ~US$20bn, supported in part by monetizing external sales 
opportunities—similar to Google’s approach with TPU. We view this as a constructive 
signal for the broader AWS AI ASIC supply chain, with Accton positioned as a potential 
beneficiary.

OpenAI announced an 8-year compute procurement deal to purchase ~2GW of 
Trainium capacity (including both T3 and T4 skus) through AWS infrastructure, to 
support its general and advanced workloads (link). Additionally, following the Project 
Rainier with ~1mn Trainium 2 procurement by the end of last year, Anthropic signed a 
new agreement with Amazon to secure up to 5GW of new compute, including a total of 
1GW Trainium 2 and Trainium 3 capacity by the end of this year (link). The 10-year 
procurement plan also includes Graviton and Trainium 4 chips, and subsequent-
generation chips.

Based on our sensitivity analysis below, each GW procurement deal for AWS’s 
Trainium chips (T3 in our exercise) represents ~US$2.1bn of incremental revenue TAM 
for Accton, comprising ~US$1.4bn for chip modules and ~US$700mn for switch trays. 
Assuming ~80%/~50% m/s for Accton in ASIC modules and NRL (NeuronLink) switch 
trays, respectively, we estimate ~US$1.4bn of incremental revenue for Accton—
equivalent to 18% of Accton’s total revenue in 2025.

Potential m/s shift in Trn3; scale-up switch tray wins could 
largely offset the downside 
Our industry research suggests AWS is onboarding additional ODM suppliers (e.g., 
Fabrinet, Wiwynn, Flextronics) for Trn3 OAM/UBB programs, which could drive 
market share dilution versus prior generations. That said, we expect Accton to retain its 
leadership position, maintaining 60%+ m/s over the next 2-3 years. In our view, the 
incremental scope from the Trn3 scale-up switch tray program could offset much of the 
potential downside from any share shift in OAM/UBB.

Lower board content per chip, partially offset by PCB material 
upgrades and potential new content gains 
Trainium 3’s liquid-cooled compute board architecture moves to four ASICs per board 
versus two ASICs per board in Trainium 2. While this mechanically lowers board 
content per ASIC, we estimate that liquid cooling adoption will remain limited at ~20% 
for the Trainium 3 generation this year.

We also see offsetting content upgrades in UBB boards, led by copper foil material 
migration (HVLP 2 to HVLP 4) and potential incremental content wins (e.g., PCIe 
switch). Overall, we estimate broadly stable OAM+UBB content per chip for Accton at 
~US$2,000 this year, but model a gradual decline in 2027/2028 as liquid cooling 
penetration increases.

III. Growing TAM opportunity through vertical 
integration 
Marching toward the server space 
Accton is increasingly positioning itself as a rack-level AI infrastructure partner, aiming 
to offer an end-to-end solution spanning both switching and server domains. Its 
portfolio extends from switch systems, NICs, optical transceivers, and cables to 
accelerator modules—and ultimately server systems.

From a content perspective, networking typically represents less than 10% of CSP cloud 
capex, while server spend can account for ~50–60%, implying meaningful expansion 
runway if Accton can scale beyond switching into servers. Management expects an 
initial contribution from the server business in 2H26, starting with smaller-scale 
customers.

Figure 42: Accton’s vision of vertical integration in datacenter networking and compute

Source: Accton’s presentation.

Gauging the potential server revenue upside 
Although Accton’s server business remains nascent, we believe the company can 
leverage its track record in switch system design and manufacturing. In the section 
below, we frame the potential upside should Accton achieve a meaningful server-side 
breakthrough.

We expect Accton to begin with white-box, general-purpose servers for smaller-scale 
customers to build a track record and establish manufacturing credibility. Accordingly, 
we exclude high-end AI server platforms (e.g., NVL72, HGX, AI ASIC servers) from 
the TAM framework below.

29

### Page 30

Albert Hung AC 
(886-2) 2725-9875 
albert.hung@jpmchase.com 
Asia Pacific Equity Research 
25 June 2026

Assuming a ~US$12k ASP for general-purpose servers and that white-box U.S. CSP 
servers represent ~50% of total general-purpose server shipments, we estimate an 
addressable server revenue TAM of ~US$74bn for Accton. Given these assumptions, 
each 1% market share gain in the U.S. CSP general-purpose server market could 
translate into ~9% upside to Accton’s 2025 revenue. Importantly, there is additional 
upside if Accton can ultimately expand into the AI server market.

Promising opportunities in TAM expansion 
Beyond servers, we see meaningful TAM expansion potential for Accton across both 
components and switch systems.

Component level: upside from optics and other critical rack-level components 
Accton’s wholly owned subsidiary, Edgecore, offers optical transceivers and cable 
products that customers can select alongside its switching solutions. Accton has also 
established Goldilink to develop 800G optical solutions to support next-generation high- 
speed data transmission. Beyond optics, Accton is positioning itself as a broader system 
integrator, with ambitions to supply other critical non-optical rack-level components, 
such as liquid cooling. For instance, Accton designs its own L2A/L2L liquid-cooling 
systems for its high-end datacenter switches.

LCOS (Liquid Crystal on Silicon) 
technology is a high-resolution optical 
device that is used in Wavelength 
Selective Switches (WSS) and 
Reconfigurable Optical Add-drop 
Multiplexers (ROADMs), which steers 
specific light wavelengths by applying 
voltage patterns to liquid crystal cells and 
enabling dynamic, flexible bandwidth 
control without moving parts. Key 
advantages of LCOS switches over 
MEMS (Micro-Electro-Mechanical 
Systems) include scalability (high radix), 
low latency, and power efficiency. 
Source: Company data.

Networking switch system level: expansion into scale-up and scale-across switches

Today, Accton’s networking business is primarily focused on scale-out switching (i.e., 
ToR switches). That said, the company has been engaging with a key U.S. CSP 
customer on a customized scale-up switch (switch tray) for ASIC training clusters, and 
we expect an initial revenue contribution beginning in 2H26.

Looking further ahead, Accton is also targeting scale-across/DCI switching, which 
enables interconnection between data center clusters. The company is focusing on 
LCoS-based WSS (Wavelength Selective Switch) and appears well positioned in this 
technology. Accton announced a US$29mn investment in InLC Technology (private) for 
LCoS capability in November last year.

Accton/Edgecore has announced collaborations with Taiwanese and Japanese telcos

hyperscalers on optical switching products.

Overall, we believe these emerging technologies and products have the potential to 
become new growth drivers for Accton, contributing a high-single-digit to low-double-
digit of total revenue in the next 1-2 years.

Key downside risks 
Potential m/s shift in Ethernet switch: competition with Nvidia 
Our research indicates that customers have shifted to Ethernet-based switches from 
Infiniband switches over the past 3 years, leading to Infiniband adoption declining to 
below one-third of the AI datacenter switch market this year from a dominant solution 
(~80%) 3 years ago. We attribute this to a step-up in Ethernet switch configuration to 
capture the AI networking switch TAM. Nvidia also rolled out its first high-performance 
Ethernet switch product line - Spectrum switch (SN5000 series) in 2023, followed by 
subsequent line-ups (i.e. Spectrum-X Photonics, SN6000 series).

As Neoclouds typically lack in-house engineering resources, they tend to follow 
Nvidia’s reference designs when building GPU server networking clusters. As a result, 
Nvidia has been gaining share in the data center Ethernet switch market over the past 
two years, supported by fast-growing neocloud AI demand. However, hyperscalers are 
used to adopting in-house networking architectures for AI clusters and outsourcing bare-
metal Ethernet switches to white-box ODM vendors such as Celestica and Accton.

While we see CSPs (e.g., Meta) adopting Nvidia’s Spectrum switch silicon in certain 
deployments versus Broadcom alternatives, CSPs still co-develop their in-house system 
designs with white-box switch ODM partners. We therefore expect bare-metal switches

32

### Page 33

Albert Hung AC 
(886-2) 2725-9875 
albert.hung@jpmchase.com 
Asia Pacific Equity Research 
25 June 2026

to remain the dominant form factor in CSP data centers at the ToR layer, given their 
meaningful cost advantage. We also see potential opportunity for whitebox Ethernet 
scale-up AI datacenter switch adoption in CSPs’ ASIC clusters, which could be an 
incremental market for Accton.

Notably, the industry economics also reinforce this structure: branded Ethernet switch 
vendors (e.g., Arista, Cisco, Juniper, Nvidia) typically generate 50%+ GMs, versus 
~15–25% GMs for switch ODMs.

Faster-than-expected m/s shift in AWS ASIC module biz 
As Amazon’s ASIC module program scales, we are seeing new entrants increasingly 
penetrate the AWS AI ASIC module supply chain, including Flextronics, Wiwynn, and 
Fabrinet. Fabrinet has already begun T2 ASIC module production in 2H25, while our 
industry research suggests Wiwynn could enter via the T3 program (MP in 2H26). 
Flextronics also appears to be a credible potential entrant.

Despite this share dilution risk, we expect Accton to retain its leadership position and 
NPI role (60%+) given its long-standing partnership with Amazon. Moreover, we 
believe ongoing content expansion—driven by PCB/CCL material upgrades, higher 
layer counts, and rising liquid-cooling adoption—along with incremental project scope 
wins (e.g., NRL switch trays) can more than offset the headwind from a lower module 
share. Overall, we continue to expect Accton’s NAE segment to deliver sustained 
revenue growth, with Amazon remaining the largest contributor.
